Provides pattern matching logic for routing rules: regex/glob patterns\n4\t//! against model names, first-match-wins semantics, and default fallback.\n5\t\n6\tuse crate::config::RoutingRule;\n7\tuse std::sync::Arc;\n8\t\n9\t/// Compiled routing rule with cached regex for efficient matching.\n10\t#[derive(Debug, Clone)]\n11\tstruct CompiledRule {\n12\t    /// Compiled regex matcher.\n13\t    matcher: Arc<regex::Regex>,\n14\t    /// Adapter to use on match.\n15\t    adapter: String,\n16\t}\n17\t\n18\timpl CompiledRule {\n19\t    /// Compile a routing rule into an efficient matcher.\n20\t    ///\n21\t    /// Supports both regex patterns (any valid regex) and glob-style patterns:\n22\t    /// - `*` matches any single path segment (non-greedy `[^/]+`)\n23\t    /// - `**` matches zero or more path segments (`.+?` with greedy quantifier)\n24\t    /// - Literal `*` or `**` must be escaped as `\\*` or `\\*\\*`\n25\t    fn from_rule(rule: &RoutingRule) -> Result<Self, regex::Error> {\n26\t        let pattern = &rule.match_model;\n27\t        let adapter = rule.adapter.clone();\n28\t\n29\t        // Convert glob patterns to regex if needed.\n30\t        // If the pattern contains * or ** without proper regex escaping,\n31\t        // treat it as a glob pattern and convert.\n32\t        let regex_pattern = if needs_glob_conversion(pattern) {\n33\t            convert_glob_to_regex(pattern)\n34\t        } else {\n35\t            pattern.clone()\n36\t        };\n37\t\n38\t        let matcher = regex::Regex::new(&regex_pattern)?;\n39\t\n40\t        Ok(CompiledRule {\n41\t            matcher: Arc::new(matcher),\n42\t            adapter,\n43\t        })\n44\t    }\n45\t\n46\t    /// Test if a model name matches this rule.\n47\t    fn matches(&self, model: &str) -> bool {\n48\t        self.matcher.is_match(model)\n49\t    }\n50\t}\n51\t\n52\t/// Check if a pattern is a glob pattern.\n53\t///\n54\t/// A simple heuristic that returns true if the pattern contains glob-style\n55\t/// wildcards (`*` or `**`). This is a basic check that doesn't attempt to\n56\t/// distinguish between glob and regex patterns - it just reports whether\n57\t/// the pattern contains wildcard characters.\n58\t///\n59\t/// # Arguments\n60\t///\n61\t/// * `pattern` - The pattern string to check.\n62\t///\n63\t/// # Returns\n64\t///\n65\t/// * `true` if the pattern contains `*` or `**`.\n66\t/// * `false` otherwise.\n67\t///\n68\t/// # Examples\n69\t///\n70\t/// ```\n71\t/// use needle::routing::is_glob_pattern;\n72\t///\n73\t/// // Glob patterns with wildcards\n74\t/// assert!(is_glob_pattern(\"*\"));\n75\t/// assert!(is_glob_pattern(\"**\"));\n76\t/// assert!(is_glob_pattern(\"gpt-*\"));\n77\t/// assert!(is_glob_pattern(\"test/**\"));\n78\t///\n79\t/// // Non-glob patterns (no wildcards)\n80\t/// assert!(!is_glob_pattern(\"exact-match\"));\n81\t/// assert!(!is_glob_pattern(\"gpt-4\"));\n82\t/// assert!(!is_glob_pattern(\"claude-sonnet\"));\n83\t/// ```\n84\tpub fn is_glob_pattern(pattern: &str) -> bool {\n85\t    pattern.contains('*')\n86\t}\n87\t\n88\t/// Check if a pattern contains glob-style wildcards that need conversion.\n89\t///\n90\t/// Returns true if the pattern appears to be a glob pattern rather than\n91\t/// a well-formed regex. The heuristic checks for:\n92\t/// 1. Regex features: anchors (^$), character classes [], parentheses (),\n93\t///    quantifiers +?{}, alternation |, or the regex wildcard sequence .*\n94\t/// 2. If present, it's a regex pattern - no conversion needed\n95\t/// 3. If absent, but * is present, it's a glob pattern - convert it\n96\tfn needs_glob_conversion(pattern: &str) -> bool {\n97\t    // First, check if it looks like a regex by looking for regex metacharacters.\n98\t    // The key indicator is .* which is \"any character\" in regex but not in glob.\n99\t    let has_regex_features = pattern.contains(|c| {\n100\t        matches!(\n101\t            c,\n102\t            '^' | '$' | '(' | ')' | '[' | ']' | '{' | '}' | '+' | '?' | '|' | '\\\\'\n103\t        )\n104\t    }) || pattern.contains(\".*\");\n105\t\n106\t    if has_regex_features {\n107\t        // Has clear regex features - treat as regex\n108\t        return false;\n109\t    }\n110\t\n111\t    // At this point, we have a simple pattern that might be glob or regex.\n112\t    // If it contains unescaped * (and we already ruled out other regex chars),\n113\t    // treat it as a glob pattern.\n114\t    let chars: Vec<char> = pattern.chars().collect();\n115\t    let mut i = 0;\n116\t\n117\t    while i < chars.len() {\n118\t        if chars[i] == '\\\\' && i + 1 < chars.len() {\n119\t            // Skip escaped character.\n120\t            i += 2;\n121\t            continue;\n122\t        }\n123\t        if chars[i] == '*' {\n124\t            return true;\n125\t        }\n126\t        i += 1;\n127\t    }\n128\t\n129\t    false\n130\t}\n131\t\n132\t/// Convert glob-style pattern to regex.\n133\t///\n134\t/// Rules:\n135\t/// - `*` alone → `.*` (match anything, catch-all)\n136\t/// - `*` in other contexts → `[^/]+` (match any single segment, no slashes)\n137\t/// - `**` → `.*` (match any characters including slashes, greedy)\n138\t/// - Escaped `\\*` and `\\*\\*` treated literally\n139\t/// - No implicit start anchor for glob patterns (unlike regex patterns)\n140\tfn convert_glob_to_regex(glob: &str) -> String {\n141\t    // Special case: * and ** should match anything (including empty).\n142\t    if glob == \"*\" || glob == \"**\" {\n143\t        return \"^.*$\".to_string();\n144\t    }\n145\t\n146\t    let mut result = String::new();\n147\t    let chars: Vec<char> = glob.chars().collect();\n148\t    let mut i = 0;\n149\t\n150\t    while i < chars.len() {\n151\t        match chars[i] {\n152\t            '\\\\' => {\n153\t                // Escaped character - pass through the backslash and the character.\n154\t                // This ensures that \\* in the glob becomes \\* in the regex (literal asterisk).\n155\t                if i + 1 < chars.len() {\n156\t                    result.push('\\\\');\n157\t                    result.push(chars[i + 1]);\n158\t                    i += 2;\n159\t                } else {\n160\t                    // Trailing backslash - treat literally.\n161\t                    result.push('\\\\');\n162\t                    i += 1;\n163\t                }\n164\t            }\n165\t            '*' => {\n166\t                // Check for **.\n167\t                if i + 1 < chars.len() && chars[i + 1] == '*' {\n168\t                    // ** matches any characters including slashes.\n169\t                    result.push_str(\".*\");\n170\t                    i += 2;\n171\t                } else {\n172\t                    // * matches any non-slash characters.\n173\t                    result.push_str(\"[^/]+\");\n174\t                    i += 1;\n175\t                }\n176\t            }\n177\t            c => {\n178\t                // Pass through regex metacharacters literally - regex::Regex::new\n179\t                // will handle escaping. We only need to escape backslashes for\n180\t                // the glob conversion.\n181\t                result.push(c);\n182\t                i += 1;\n183\t            }\n184\t        }\n185\t    }\n186\t\n187\t    // Only add end anchor, not start anchor (glob patterns match substrings).\n188\t    format!(\"{}$\", result)\n189\t}\n190\t\n191\t/// Match a model name against routing rules, returning the adapter to use.\n192\t///\n193\t/// Evaluates rules in order; the first matching rule determines the adapter.\n194\t/// If no rule matches, returns the default adapter.\n195\t///\n196\t/// # Arguments\n197\t///\n198\t/// * `model` - The model name to match (e.g., \"claude-sonnet-4-6\").\n199\t/// * `rules` - Ordered list of routing rules (first match wins).\n200\t/// * `default` - Fallback adapter when no rules match.\n201\t///\n202\t/// # Returns\n203\t///\n204\t/// * `Some(adapter)` - The adapter name to use.\n205\t/// * `None` - No rule matched and default is empty/invalid (caller should handle).\n206\t///\n207\t/// # Examples\n208\t///\n209\t/// ```\n210\t/// use needle::routing::match_adapter;\n211\t/// use needle::config::RoutingRule;\n212\t///\n213\t/// let rules = vec![\n214\t///     RoutingRule {\n215\t///         match_model: \"sonnet.*\".to_string(),\n216\t///         adapter: \"claude-print\".to_string(),\n217\t///     },\n218\t///     RoutingRule {\n219\t///         match_model: \"*\".to_string(),\n220\t///         adapter: \"default-adapter\".to_string(),\n221\t///     },\n222\t/// ];\n223\t///\n224\t/// // First rule matches.\n225\t/// assert_eq!(\n226\t///     match_adapter(\"sonnet-4-6\", &rules, \"fallback\"),\n227\t///     Some(\"claude-print\".to_string())\n228\t/// );\n229\t///\n230\t/// // Second rule (catch-all) matches.\n231\t/// assert_eq!(\n232\t///     match_adapter(\"other-model\", &rules, \"fallback\"),\n233\t///     Some(\"default-adapter\".to_string())\n234\t/// );\n235\t/// ```\n236\tpub fn match_adapter(model: &str, rules: &[RoutingRule], default: &str) -> Option<String> {\n237\t    // Compile rules and test in order (first match wins).\n238\t    for rule in rules {\n239\t        match CompiledRule::from_rule(rule) {\n240\t            Ok(compiled) => {\n241\t                if compiled.matches(model) {\n242\t                    return Some(compiled.adapter.clone());\n243\t                }\n244\t            }\n245\t            Err(e) => {\n246\t                // Log the error but continue with other rules.\n247\t                // Invalid patterns are skipped rather than failing the entire dispatch.\n248\t                tracing::warn!(\n249\t                    pattern = %rule.match_model,\n250\t                    error = %e,\n251\t                    \"invalid routing pattern — skipping rule\"\n252\t                );\n253\t            }\n254\t        }\n255\t    }\n256\t\n257\t    // No rule matched — use default if provided.\n258\t    if default.is_empty() {\n259\t        None\n260\t    } else {\n261\t        Some(default.to_string())\n262\t    }\n263\t}\n264\t\n265\t/// Match a model name against a single glob pattern using the glob crate.\n266\t///\n267\t/// This is a lower-level function that tests if a glob pattern matches a model name.\n268\t/// It uses the `glob` crate's Pattern type directly for glob-style pattern matching.\n269\t///\n270\t/// # Arguments\n271\t///\n272\t/// * `pattern` - Glob pattern to match (e.g., \"claude-*\", \"gpt-*\", \"*\").\n273\t/// * `model_name` - The model name to test against the pattern.\n274\t///\n275\t/// # Returns\n276\t///\n277\t/// * `Some(())` - The pattern matches the model name.\n278\t/// * `None` - The pattern does not match or is invalid.\n279\t///\n280\t/// # Edge Cases\n281\t///\n282\t/// * Empty pattern: Returns `None` (no match).\n283\t/// * Empty model name: Returns `None` (no match).\n284\t/// * Invalid glob pattern: Returns `None` (no match).\n285\t///\n286\t/// # Glob Pattern Syntax\n287\t///\n288\t/// * `*` - Matches any sequence of non-separator characters.\n289\t/// * `**` - Matches any sequence of characters, including path separators.\n290\t/// * `?` - Matches any single non-separator character.\n291\t/// * `[a-z]` - Matches any character in the bracket.\n292\t/// * `[!a-z]` - Matches any character not in the bracket.\n293\t///\n294\t/// # Examples\n295\t///\n296\t/// ```\n297\t/// use needle::routing::match_adapter_with_glob;\n298\t///\n299\t/// // Match with wildcard\n300\t/// assert!(match_adapter_with_glob(\"claude-*\", \"claude-sonnet-4-6\").is_some());\n301\t/// assert!(match_adapter_with_glob(\"claude-*\", \"claude-opus-4-6\").is_some());\n302\t/// assert!(match_adapter_with_glob(\"claude-*\", \"gpt-4\").is_none());\n303\t///\n304\t/// // Match with double wildcard\n305\t/// assert!(match_adapter_with_glob(\"**\", \"any-model\").is_some());\n306\t///\n307\t/// // Edge cases\n308\t/// assert!(match_adapter_with_glob(\"\", \"model\").is_none());\n309\t/// assert!(match_adapter_with_glob(\"pattern\", \"\").is_none());\n310\t/// ```\n311\tpub fn match_adapter_with_glob(pattern: &str, model_name: &str) -> Option<()> {\n312\t    // Handle edge cases\n313\t    if pattern.is_empty() || model_name.is_empty() {\n314\t        return None;\n315\t    }\n316\t\n317\t    // Use the glob crate to compile and match the pattern\n318\t    match glob::Pattern::new(pattern) {\n319\t        Ok(glob_pattern) => {\n320\t            // The glob crate's Pattern::matches takes a &str directly\n321\t            if glob_pattern.matches(model_name) {\n322\t                Some(())\n323\t            } else {\n324\t                None\n325\t            }\n326\t        }\n327\t        Err(_) => {\n328\t            // Invalid glob pattern\n329\t            None\n330\t        }\n331\t    }\n332\t}\n333\t\n334\t/// Match a model name against a glob pattern.\n335\t///\n336\t/// This function uses the `glob` crate's Pattern functionality to perform\n337\t/// glob-style pattern matching against model names.\n338\t///\n339\t/// # Arguments\n340\t///\n341\t/// * `pattern` - Glob pattern to match (e.g., \"claude-*\", \"gpt-*\", \"*\").\n342\t/// * `model_name` - The model name to test against the pattern.\n343\t///\n344\t/// # Returns\n345\t///\n346\t/// * `true` - The pattern matches the model name.\n347\t/// * `false` - The pattern does not match, pattern is invalid, or either argument is empty.\n348\t///\n349\t/// # Glob Pattern Syntax\n350\t///\n351\t/// * `*` - Matches any sequence of non-separator characters.\n352\t/// * `**` - Matches any sequence of characters, including path separators.\n353\t/// * `?` - Matches any single non-separator character.\n354\t/// * `[a-z]` - Matches any character in the bracket.\n355\t/// * `[!a-z]` - Matches any character not in the bracket.\n356\t///\n357\t/// # Examples\n358\t///\n359\t/// ```\n360\t/// use needle::routing::match_glob;\n361\t///\n362\t/// // Match with wildcard\n363\t/// assert!(match_glob(\"claude-*\", \"claude-sonnet-4-6\"));\n364\t/// assert!(match_glob(\"claude-*\", \"claude-opus-4-6\"));\n365\t/// assert!(!match_glob(\"claude-*\", \"gpt-4\"));\n366\t///\n367\t/// // Match with double wildcard\n368\t/// assert!(match_glob(\"**\", \"any-model\"));\n369\t///\n370\t/// // Edge cases\n371\t/// assert!(!match_glob(\"\", \"model\"));  // Empty pattern\n372\t/// assert!(!match_glob(\"pattern\", \"\"));  // Empty model name\n373\t/// ```\n374\tpub fn match_glob(pattern: &str, model_name: &str) -> bool {\n375\t    // Handle edge cases\n376\t    if pattern.is_empty() || model_name.is_empty() {\n377\t        return false;\n378\t    }\n379\t\n380\t    // Use the glob crate to compile and match the pattern\n381\t    match glob::Pattern::new(pattern) {\n382\t        Ok(glob_pattern) => glob_pattern.matches(model_name),\n383\t        Err(_) => false, // Invalid glob pattern\n384\t    }\n385\t}\n386\t\n387\t// assert!(match_glob(\"gpt-[!0-9]\", \"gpt-x\"));\n1472\t        assert!(!match_glob(\"gpt-[!0-9]\", \"gpt-4\"));\n1473\t\n1474\t        // Test ranges\n1475\t        assert!(match_glob(\"model-[a-c]\", \"model-d\"));\n1479\t    }\n1480\t}\n1481\t"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"da985e7d-0be2-4d62-acd6-938015ca67cf","uuid":"4ef6dddc-1579-49d6-8311-810e342ebf0e","timestamp":"2026-07-28T21:10:25.714Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/home/coding/NEEDLE/src/routing.rs","content":"//! Routing rule matcher for model-to-adapter dispatch.\n//!\n//! Provides pattern matching logic for routing rules: regex/glob patterns\n//! against model names, first-match-wins semantics, and default fallback.\n\nuse crate::config::RoutingRule;\nuse std::sync::Arc;\n\n/// Compiled routing rule with cached regex for efficient matching.\n#[derive(Debug, Clone)]\nstruct CompiledRule {\n    /// Compiled regex matcher.\n    matcher: Arc<regex::Regex>,\n    /// Adapter to use on match.\n    adapter: String,\n}\n\nimpl CompiledRule {\n    /// Compile a routing rule into an efficient matcher.\n    ///\n    /// Supports both regex patterns (any valid regex) and glob-style patterns:\n    /// - `*` matches any single path segment (non-greedy `[^/]+`)\n    /// - `**` matches zero or more path segments (`.+?` with greedy quantifier)\n    /// - Literal `*` or `**` must be escaped as `\\*` or `\\*\\*`\n    fn from_rule(rule: &RoutingRule) -> Result<Self, regex::Error> {\n        let pattern = &rule.match_model;\n        let adapter = rule.adapter.clone();\n\n        // Convert glob patterns to regex if needed.\n        // If the pattern contains * or ** without proper regex escaping,\n        // treat it as a glob pattern and convert.\n        let regex_pattern = if needs_glob_conversion(pattern) {\n            convert_glob_to_regex(pattern)\n        } else {\n            pattern.clone()\n        };\n\n        let matcher = regex::Regex::new(&regex_pattern)?;\n\n        Ok(CompiledRule {\n            matcher: Arc::new(matcher),\n            adapter,\n        })\n    }\n\n    /// Test if a model name matches this rule.\n    fn matches(&self, model: &str) -> bool {\n        self.matcher.is_match(model)\n    }\n}\n\n/// Check if a pattern is a glob pattern.\n///\n/// A simple heuristic that returns true if the pattern contains glob-style\n/// wildcards (`*` or `**`). This is a basic check that doesn't attempt to\n/// distinguish between glob and regex patterns - it just reports whether\n/// the pattern contains wildcard characters.\n///\n/// # Arguments\n///\n/// * `pattern` - The pattern string to check.\n///\n/// # Returns\n///\n/// * `true` if the pattern contains `*` or `**`.\n/// * `false` otherwise.\n///\n/// # Examples\n///\n/// ```\n/// use needle::routing::is_glob_pattern;\n///\n/// // Glob patterns with wildcards\n/// assert!(is_glob_pattern(\"*\"));\n/// assert!(is_glob_pattern(\"**\"));\n/// assert!(is_glob_pattern(\"gpt-*\"));\n/// assert!(is_glob_pattern(\"test/**\"));\n///\n/// // Non-glob patterns (no wildcards)\n/// assert!(!is_glob_pattern(\"exact-match\"));\n/// assert!(!is_glob_pattern(\"gpt-4\"));\n/// assert!(!is_glob_pattern(\"claude-sonnet\"));\n/// ```\npub fn is_glob_pattern(pattern: &str) -> bool {\n    pattern.contains('*')\n}\n\n/// Check if a pattern contains glob-style wildcards that need conversion.\n///\n/// Returns true if the pattern appears to be a glob pattern rather than\n/// a well-formed regex. The heuristic checks for:\n/// 1. Regex features: anchors (^$), character classes [], parentheses (),\n///    quantifiers +?{}, alternation |, or the regex wildcard sequence .*\n/// 2. If present, it's a regex pattern - no conversion needed\n/// 3. If absent, but * is present, it's a glob pattern - convert it\nfn needs_glob_conversion(pattern: &str) -> bool {\n    // First, check if it looks like a regex by looking for regex metacharacters.\n    // The key indicator is .* which is \"any character\" in regex but not in glob.\n    let has_regex_features = pattern.contains(|c| {\n        matches!(\n            c,\n            '^' | '$' | '(' | ')' | '[' | ']' | '{' | '}' | '+' | '?' | '|' | '\\\\'\n        )\n    }) || pattern.contains(\".*\");\n\n    if has_regex_features {\n        // Has clear regex features - treat as regex\n        return false;\n    }\n\n    // At this point, we have a simple pattern that might be glob or regex.\n    // If it contains unescaped * (and we already ruled out other regex chars),\n    // treat it as a glob pattern.\n    let chars: Vec<char> = pattern.chars().collect();\n    let mut i = 0;\n\n    while i < chars.len() {\n        if chars[i] == '\\\\' && i + 1 < chars.len() {\n            // Skip escaped character.\n            i += 2;\n            continue;\n        }\n        if chars[i] == '*' {\n            return true;\n        }\n        i += 1;\n    }\n\n    false\n}\n\n/// Convert glob-style pattern to regex.\n///\n/// Rules:\n/// - `*` alone → `.*` (match anything, catch-all)\n/// - `*` in other contexts → `[^/]+` (match any single segment, no slashes)\n/// - `**` → `.*` (match any characters including slashes, greedy)\n/// - Escaped `\\*` and `\\*\\*` treated literally\n/// - No implicit start anchor for glob patterns (unlike regex patterns)\nfn convert_glob_to_regex(glob: &str) -> String {\n    // Special case: * and ** should match anything (including empty).\n    if glob == \"*\" || glob == \"**\" {\n        return \"^.*$\".to_string();\n    }\n\n    let mut result = String::new();\n    let chars: Vec<char> = glob.chars().collect();\n    let mut i = 0;\n\n    while i < chars.len() {\n        match chars[i] {\n            '\\\\' => {\n                // Escaped character - pass through the backslash and the character.\n                // This ensures that \\* in the glob becomes \\* in the regex (literal asterisk).\n                if i + 1 < chars.len() {\n                    result.push('\\\\');\n                    result.push(chars[i + 1]);\n                    i += 2;\n                } else {\n                    // Trailing backslash - treat literally.\n                    result.push('\\\\');\n                    i += 1;\n                }\n            }\n            '*' => {\n                // Check for **.\n                if i + 1 < chars.len() && chars[i + 1] == '*' {\n                    // ** matches any characters including slashes.\n                    result.push_str(\".*\");\n                    i += 2;\n                } else {\n                    // * matches any non-slash characters.\n                    result.push_str(\"[^/]+\");\n                    i += 1;\n                }\n            }\n            c => {\n                // Pass through regex metacharacters literally - regex::Regex::new\n                // will handle escaping. We only need to escape backslashes for\n                // the glob conversion.\n                result.push(c);\n                i += 1;\n            }\n        }\n    }\n\n    // Only add end anchor, not start anchor (glob patterns match substrings).\n    format!(\"{}$\", result)\n}\n\n/// Match a model name against routing rules, returning the adapter to use.\n///\n/// Evaluates rules in order; the first matching rule determines the adapter.\n/// If no rule matches, returns the default adapter.\n///\n/// # Arguments\n///\n/// * `model` - The model name to match (e.g., \"claude-sonnet-4-6\").\n/// * `rules` - Ordered list of routing rules (first match wins).\n/// * `default` - Fallback adapter when no rules match.\n///\n/// # Returns\n///\n/// * `Some(adapter)` - The adapter name to use.\n/// * `None` - No rule matched and default is empty/invalid (caller should handle).\n///\n/// # Examples\n///\n/// ```\n/// use needle::routing::match_adapter;\n/// use needle::config::RoutingRule;\n///\n/// let rules = vec![\n///     RoutingRule {\n///         match_model: \"sonnet.*\".to_string(),\n///         adapter: \"claude-print\".to_string(),\n///     },\n///     RoutingRule {\n///         match_model: \"*\".to_string(),\n///         adapter: \"default-adapter\".to_string(),\n///     },\n/// ];\n///\n/// // First rule matches.\n/// assert_eq!(\n///     match_adapter(\"sonnet-4-6\", &rules, \"fallback\"),\n///     Some(\"claude-print\".to_string())\n/// );\n///\n/// // Second rule (catch-all) matches.\n/// assert_eq!(\n///     match_adapter(\"other-model\", &rules, \"fallback\"),\n///     Some(\"default-adapter\".to_string())\n/// );\n/// ```\npub fn match_adapter(model: &str, rules: &[RoutingRule], default: &str) -> Option<String> {\n    // Compile rules and test in order (first match wins).\n    for rule in rules {\n        match CompiledRule::from_rule(rule) {\n            Ok(compiled) => {\n                if compiled.matches(model) {\n                    return Some(compiled.adapter.clone());\n                }\n            }\n            Err(e) => {\n                // Log the error but continue with other rules.\n                // Invalid patterns are skipped rather than failing the entire dispatch.\n                tracing::warn!(\n                    pattern = %rule.match_model,\n                    error = %e,\n                    \"invalid routing pattern — skipping rule\"\n                );\n            }\n        }\n    }\n\n    // No rule matched — use default if provided.\n    if default.is_empty() {\n        None\n    } else {\n        Some(default.to_string())\n    }\n}\n\n/// Match a model name against a single glob pattern using the glob crate.\n///\n/// This is a lower-level function that tests if a glob pattern matches a model name.\n/// It uses the `glob` crate's Pattern type directly for glob-style pattern matching.\n///\n/// # Arguments\n///\n/// * `pattern` - Glob pattern to match (e.g., \"claude-*\", \"gpt-*\", \"*\").\n/// * `model_name` - The model name to test against the pattern.\n///\n/// # Returns\n///\n/// * `Some(())` - The pattern matches the model name.\n/// * `None` - The pattern does not match or is invalid.\n///\n/// # Edge Cases\n///\n/// * Empty pattern: Returns `None` (no match).\n/// * Empty model name: Returns `None` (no match).\n/// * Invalid glob pattern: Returns `None` (no match).\n///\n/// # Glob Pattern Syntax\n///\n/// * `*` - Matches any sequence of non-separator characters.\n/// * `**` - Matches any sequence of characters, including path separators.\n/// * `?` - Matches any single non-separator character.\n/// * `[a-z]` - Matches any character in the bracket.\n/// * `[!a-z]` - Matches any character not in the bracket.\n///\n/// # Examples\n///\n/// ```\n/// use needle::routing::match_adapter_with_glob;\n///\n/// // Match with wildcard\n/// assert!(match_adapter_with_glob(\"claude-*\", \"claude-sonnet-4-6\").is_some());\n/// assert!(match_adapter_with_glob(\"claude-*\", \"claude-opus-4-6\").is_some());\n/// assert!(match_adapter_with_glob(\"claude-*\", \"gpt-4\").is_none());\n///\n/// // Match with double wildcard\n/// assert!(match_adapter_with_glob(\"**\", \"any-model\").is_some());\n///\n/// // Edge cases\n/// assert!(match_adapter_with_glob(\"\", \"model\").is_none());\n/// assert!(match_adapter_with_glob(\"pattern\", \"\").is_none());\n/// ```\npub fn match_adapter_with_glob(pattern: &str, model_name: &str) -> Option<()> {\n    // Handle edge cases\n    if pattern.is_empty() || model_name.is_empty() {\n        return None;\n    }\n\n    // Use the glob crate to compile and match the pattern\n    match glob::Pattern::new(pattern) {\n        Ok(glob_pattern) => {\n            // The glob crate's Pattern::matches takes a &str directly\n            if glob_pattern.matches(model_name) {\n                Some(())\n            } else {\n                None\n            }\n        }\n        Err(_) => {\n            // Invalid glob pattern\n            None\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n/// Match a model name against a glob pattern.\n///\n/// This function uses the `glob` crate's Pattern functionality to perform\n/// glob-style pattern matching against model names.\n///\n/// # Arguments\n///\n/// * `pattern` - Glob pattern to match (e.g., \"claude-*\", \"gpt-*\", \"*\").\n/// * `model_name` - The model name to test against the pattern.\n///\n/// # Returns\n///\n/// * `true` - The pattern matches the model name.\n/// * `false` - The pattern does not match, pattern is invalid, or either argument is empty.\n///\n/// # Glob Pattern Syntax\n///\n/// * `*` - Matches any sequence of non-separator characters.\n/// * `**` - Matches any sequence of characters, including path separators.\n/// * `?` - Matches any single non-separator character.\n/// * `[a-z]` - Matches any character in the bracket.\n/// * `[!a-z]` - Matches any character not in the bracket.\n///\n/// # Examples\n///\n/// ```\n/// use needle::routing::match_glob;\n///\n/// // Match with wildcard\n/// assert!(match_glob(\"claude-*\", \"claude-sonnet-4-6\"));\n/// assert!(match_glob(\"claude-*\", \"claude-opus-4-6\"));\n/// assert!(!match_glob(\"claude-*\", \"gpt-4\"));\n///\n/// // Match with double wildcard\n/// assert!(match_glob(\"**\", \"any-model\"));\n///\n/// // Edge cases\n/// assert!(!match_glob(\"\", \"model\"));  // Empty pattern\n/// assert!(!match_glob(\"pattern\", \"\"));  // Empty model name\n/// ```\npub fn match_glob(pattern: &str, model_name: &str) -> bool {\n    // Handle edge cases\n    if pattern.is_empty() || model_name.is_empty() {\n        return false;\n    }\n\n    // Use the glob crate to compile and match the pattern\n    match glob::Pattern::new(pattern) {\n        Ok(glob_pattern) => glob_pattern.matches(model_name),\n        Err(_) => false, // Invalid glob pattern\n    }\n}\n\n// ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n// Tests\n// ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n\n#[cfg(test)]\nmod tests {\n    use super::*;\n    use crate::config::RoutingRule;\n\n    fn make_rule(pattern: &str, match_adapter(\"provider/foo/bar/model\", &rules, \"fallback\"),\n            Some(\"adapter\".to_string())\n        );\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn is_glob_pattern_detection() {\n        // Glob patterns with wildcards\n        assert!(is_glob_pattern(\"*\"));\n        assert!(is_glob_pattern(\"**\"));\n        assert!(is_glob_pattern(\"gpt-*\"));\n        assert!(is_glob_pattern(\"test/**\"));\n\n        // Non-glob patterns (no wildcards)\n        assert!(!is_glob_pattern(\"exact-match\"));\n        assert!(!is_glob_pattern(\"gpt-4\"));\n        assert!(!is_glob_pattern(\"claude-sonnet\"));\n\n        // Mixed patterns\n        assert!(is_glob_pattern(\"claude-*\"));\n        assert!(is_glob_pattern(\"provider/**\"));\n        assert!(is_glob_pattern(\"*-sonnet-*\"));\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn needs_glob_conversion_detection() {\n        // Plain strings (no wildcards, no regex characters) don't need conversion.\n        assert!(!needs_glob_conversion(\"hello\"));\n        assert!(!needs_glob_conversion(\"model-name\"));\n        assert!(!needs_glob_conversion(\"claude_sonnet\"));\n\n        // Patterns with unescaped * need conversion.\n        assert!(needs_glob_conversion(\"pattern*\"));\n        assert!(needs_glob_conversion(\"**\"));\n        assert!(needs_glob_conversion(\"claude-*\"));\n\n        // Escaped asterisks don't need conversion.\n        assert!(!needs_glob_conversion(r\"pattern\\*\"));\n        assert!(!needs_glob_conversion(r\"\\*\\*\"));\n\n        // Regex patterns without glob characters don't need conversion.\n        assert!(!needs_glob_conversion(\"sonnet.*\"));\n        assert!(!needs_glob_conversion(\"^claude$\"));\n        assert!(!needs_glob_conversion(\"[a-z]+\"));\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn convert_glob_to_regex_single_asterisk() {\n        assert_eq!(convert_glob_to_regex(\"*\"), \"^.*$\");\n        assert_eq!(convert_glob_to_regex(\"claude-*\"), \"claude-[^/]+$\");\n        assert_eq!(convert_glob_to_regex(\"provider/*\"), \"provider/[^/]+$\");\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn convert_glob_to_regex_double_asterisk() {\n        assert_eq!(convert_glob_to_regex(\"**\"), \"^.*$\");\n        assert_eq!(convert_glob_to_regex(\"provider/**\"), \"provider/.*$\");\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn convert_glob_to_regex_escaped() {\n        assert_eq!(convert_glob_to_regex(r\"model\\*\"), r\"model\\*$\");\n        assert_eq!(convert_glob_to_regex(r\"model\\*\\*\"), r\"model\\*\\*$\");\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn convert_glob_to_regex_mixed() {\n        // Mix of glob wildcards and literal characters.\n        assert_eq!(convert_glob_to_regex(\"a*b\"), \"a[^/]+b$\");\n        assert_eq!(convert_glob_to_regex(\"a**b\"), \"a.*b$\");\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn compiled_rule_matches() {\n        let rule = CompiledRule::from_rule(&make_rule(\"sonnet.*\", \"adapter\")).unwrap();\n        assert!(rule.matches(\"sonnet-4-6\"));\n        assert!(rule.matches(\"claude-sonnet-4-6\"));\n        assert!(!rule.matches(\"opus-4-6\"));\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn compiled_rule_invalid_pattern() {\n        let result = CompiledRule::from_rule(&make_rule(\"[invalid\", \"adapter\"));\n        assert!(result.is_err());\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn real_world_anthropic_routing() {\n        // Real-world example: subscription vs API billing.\n        let rules = vec![\n            make_rule(\"(claude-)?(sonnet|opus|fable|haiku).*\", \"claude-print\"),\n            make_rule(\"*\", \"claude-code-glm-4.7\"),\n        ];\n\n        // Anthropic models -> subscription (claude-print).\n        assert_eq!(\n            match_adapter(\"claude-sonnet-4-6\", &rules, \"fallback\"),\n            Some(\"claude-print\".to_string())\n        );\n        assert_eq!(\n            match_adapter(\"claude-opus-4-6\", &rules, \"fallback\"),\n            Some(\"claude-print\".to_string())\n        );\n        assert_eq!(\n            match_adapter(\"claude-fable-5\", &rules, \"fallback\"),\n            Some(\"claude-print\".to_string())\n        );\n        assert_eq!(\n            match_adapter(\"claude-haiku-4-5-20251001\", &rules, \"fallback\"),\n            Some(\"claude-print\".to_string())\n        );\n        assert_eq!(\n            match_adapter(\"sonnet-4-6\", &rules, \"fallback\"),\n            Some(\"claude-print\".to_string())\n        );\n        assert_eq!(\n            match_adapter(\"opus-4-6\", &rules, \"fallback\"),\n            Some(\"claude-print\".to_string())\n        );\n\n        // Other models -> API billing (claude-code-glm-4.7).\n        assert_eq!(\n            match_adapter(\"gpt-4\", &rules, \"fallback\"),\n            Some(\"claude-code-glm-4.7\".to_string())\n        );\n        assert_eq!(\n            match_adapter(\"gemini-pro\", &rules, \"fallback\"),\n            Some(\"claude-code-glm-4.7\".to_string())\n        );\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn adapter_names_preserved() {\n        // &rules, \"fallback\"),\n            Some(\"Claude-Print-v2.0\".to_string())\n        );\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn empty_model_name() {\n        let rules = vec![make_rule(\"*\", \"adapter\")];\n        // Empty model should match catch-all.\n        assert_eq!(\n            match_adapter(\"\", &rules, \"fallback\"),\n            Some(\"adapter\".to_string())\n        );\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n &rules, \"fallback\"),\n            Some(\"exact-adapter\".to_string())\n        );\n\n        // Non-matching models fall back to default.\n        assert_eq!(\n            match_adapter(\"gpt-3.5\", Some(\"fallback\".to_string())\n        );\n        assert_eq!(\n            match_adapter(\"claude-sonnet\", &rules, \"fallback\"),\n            Some(\"fallback\".to_string())\n        );\n    }\n\n    // ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n    // Tests for match_adapter_with_glob\n    // ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n\n    #[test]\n    fn glob_match_with_wildcard() {\n        // Single asterisk wildcard\n        assert!(match_adapter_with_glob(\"claude-*\", \"claude-sonnet-4-6\").is_some());\n        assert!(match_adapter_with_glob(\"claude-*\", \"claude-opus-4-6\").is_some());\n        assert!(match_adapter_with_glob(\"claude-*\", \"claude-haiku-4-5\").is_some());\n        assert!(match_adapter_with_glob(\"gpt-*\", \"gpt-4\").is_some());\n        assert!(match_adapter_with_glob(\"gpt-*\", \"gpt-3.5\").is_some());\n\n        // Non-matching patterns\n        assert!(match_adapter_with_glob(\"claude-*\", \"gpt-4\").is_none());\n        assert!(match_adapter_with_glob(\"gpt-*\", \"claude-sonnet\").is_none());\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn glob_match_catchall() {\n        // Catch-all pattern with *\n        assert!(match_adapter_with_glob(\"*\", \"any-model\").is_some());\n        assert!(match_adapter_with_glob(\"*\", \"claude-sonnet-4-6\").is_some());\n        assert!(match_adapter_with_glob(\"*\", \"gpt-4\").is_some());\n        assert!(match_adapter_with_glob(\"*\", \"provider/model\").is_some());\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn glob_match_double_wildcard() {\n        // Double asterisk matches assert!(match_adapter_with_glob(\"**\", \"nested/path/model\").is_some());\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn glob_match_question_mark() {\n        // Question mark matches exactly one character\n        assert!(match_adapter_with_glob(\"gpt-?\", \"gpt-4\").is_some());\n        assert!(match_adapter_with_glob(\"gpt-?\", \"gpt-3\").is_some());\n        assert!(match_adapter_with_glob(\"gpt-?\", \"gpt-35\").is_none()); // Two chars\n        assert!(match_adapter_with_glob(\"gpt-?\", \"gpt-\").is_none()); // Zero chars after dash\n        assert!(match_adapter_with_glob(\"gpt-?\", \"gpt-4.5\").is_none()); // Has extra chars\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn glob_match_character_class() {\n        // Character class [a-z]\n        assert!(match_adapter_with_glob(\"claude-[a-z]*\",        assert!(match_adapter_with_glob(\"gpt-[0-9]\", \"gpt-35\").is_none()); // Two digits\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn glob_match_empty_pattern() {\n        // Empty pattern returns None\n assert!(match_adapter_with_glob(\"claude-sonnet-4-6\", \"claude-opus-4-6\").is_none());\n        assert!(match_adapter_with_glob(\"gpt-4\", \"gpt-3.5\").is_none());\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn assert!(match_adapter_with_glob(\"claude-*\", \"claude\").is_none()); // No suffix\n\n        assert!(match_adapter_with_glob(\"gpt-?\", \"gpt-\").is_none()); // Need one char\n \"gpt-4\").is_some());\n        assert!(match_adapter_with_glob(\"gpt-*\", \"gpt-4-turbo\").is_some());\n        assert!(match_adapter_with_glob(\"gpt-*\", \"gpt-3.5-turbo\").is_some());\n\n        assert!(match_adapter_with_glob(\"*-turbo\", \"gpt-4-turbo\").is_some());\n        assert!(match_adapter_with_glob(\"*-turbo\", \"claude-sonnet-turbo\").is_some());\n        assert!(match_adapter_with_glob(\"*-turbo\", \"gpt-4\").is_none());\n\n        // Provider/model patterns\n        assert!(match_adapter_with_glob(\"anthropic/*\", \"anthropic/claude-sonnet\").is_some());\n        assert!(match_adapter_with_glob(\"openai/*\", \"openai/gpt-4\").is_some());\n        assert!(match_adapter_with_glob(\"anthropic/*\", \"openai/gpt-4\").is_none());\n    }\n\n    // ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n    // Tests for match_glob\n    // ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n\n    #[test]\n    fn match_glob_with_wildcard() {\n        // Single asterisk wildcard\n        assert!(match_glob(\"claude-*\", \"claude-sonnet-4-6\"));\n        assert!(match_glob(\"claude-*\", \"claude-opus-4-6\"));\n        assert!(match_glob(\"claude-*\", \"claude-haiku-4-5\"));\n        assert!(match_glob(\"gpt-*\", \"gpt-4\"));\n        assert!(match_glob(\"gpt-*\", \"gpt-3.5\"));\n\n        // Non-matching patterns\n        assert!(!match_glob(\"claude-*\", \"gpt-4\"));\n        assert!(!match_glob(\"gpt-*\", \"claude-sonnet\"));\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn match_glob_catchall() {\n        // Catch-all pattern with *\n        assert!(match_glob(\"*\", \"any-model\"));\n        assert!(match_glob(\"*\", \"claude-sonnet-4-6\"));\n        assert!(match_glob(\"*\", \"gpt-4\"));\n        assert!(match_glob(\"*\", \"provider/model\"));\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn match_glob_double_wildcard() {\n        // Double asterisk matches any characters including slashes\n        assert!(match_glob(\"**\", \"any-model\"));\n        assert!(match_glob(\"**\", \"provider/model\"));\n        assert!(match_glob(\"**\", \"nested/path/model\"));\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn match_glob_question_mark() {\n        // Question mark matches exactly one character\n assert!(!match_glob(\"gpt-?\", \"gpt-\")); // Zero chars after dash\n        assert!(!match_glob(\"gpt-?\", \"gpt-4.5\")); // Has extra chars\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn match_glob_character_class() assert!(match_glob(\"gpt-[0-9]\", \"gpt-4\"));\n        assert!(!match_glob(\"gpt-[0-9]\", \"gpt-35\")); // Two digits\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn match_glob_empty_pattern() {\n        // Empty \"gpt-4\"));\n        assert!(!match_glob(\"claude-sonnet-4-6\", \"claude-opus-4-6\"));\n        assert!(!match_glob(\"gpt-4\", \"gpt-3.5\"));\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn
